Server: `AWS_SERVER_IDENTITY` (deny), `AWS_STS_URL`/`AWS_SQS_URL`, `services/aws.ts` (`configureAwsServer` with `fromNodeProviderChain`, `awsIdentityView`, `awsSettingsProblem`), `resources.awsIdentity`, `serverIdentityTarget` → `aws:<region>:<role>` for SQS and Kafka's MSK IAM (admins only; changing the role too), Git deploy refuses it where not allowed. 2b done: `aws/fake-sqs.ts` (SQS's JSON protocol in the fake AWS: standard and FIFO queues, groups blocked while in flight, dedup, visibility, receive counts, redrive to a DLQ, long polling woken by sends, delays, stale receipts deleting nothing, `redeliver`; `secondMs`); `aws/sqs.ts` `sqsClient` (`queueUrl` by name or URL, `receive`, `delete`, `changeVisibility` in tens) and `sqsConnector` (Test connection via STS + `ListQueues`, a 403 passes; the **send** query `{queue, messages}` via `SendMessageBatch` in tens, typed attributes, FIFO ids, failures naming what was sent before); the server checks SQS settings on save and **only admins set an SQS endpoint**. Decided: **a FIFO queue is received one message per call**, so SQS blocks the group while it runs (a batch may carry several of one group, which would run at once). 2c done: `queue-adapters/sqs.ts` (long poll 20 s, up to `maxInFlight` per receive or 1 for FIFO, visibility 60 s renewed every 20 s, delete on success, visibility 0 on failure and on stop, the client closed 30 s after a reset); `SqsRunMessage` and `KafkaRunMessage` in engine types (`RunInput.message` is a union); `test/sqs-consumer.test.ts` (8: a run per message and delete, failure then redrive to the DLQ, a run outlasting the visibility, FIFO order with groups alongside, `maxInFlight`, a lost delete, handover on stop, a missing queue on the trigger's state then picked up); stable over 3 repeats with Pub/Sub's. 3a done. **The spike:** `@confluentinc/kafka-javascript` 1.11.0 (librdkafka 2.16, prebuilt for darwin-arm64 and linux glibc, features ssl, sasl_plain, sasl_scram, sasl_oauthbearer) and `kafkajs` 2.2.4 both talked to `apache/kafka:4.1.0`; kafkajs warns on Node 24 (negative timeouts) and has had no release since 2023; Confluent's loads in `node:24-bookworm-slim` and its low-level `KafkaConsumer.consume(n)` matches the adapter's `pull(max)`: **chosen**. Its install script downloads the binary from GitHub (node-pre-gyp, falling back to a source build): it is in `onlyBuiltDependencies`. Built: `kafka/config.ts` (brokers, auth none|plain|scram-sha-256|scram-sha-512|mtls|aws-msk-iam, tls, username, CA, client certificate, clientId, the AWS identity's fields for MSK IAM; secrets password, clientKey, secretAccessKey; `kafkaSettingsProblem`, `rdkafkaSettings`), `kafka/client.ts` (`kafkaClient`: librdkafka settings, an admin and a producer connected on first use, **librdkafka's error log lines kept, because a wrong password or untrusted certificate comes back only as "Broker transport failure"**: `kafkaError` quotes the most telling line; `withSignal`), `kafka/connector.ts` (Test connection lists topics; the **produce** query answers each partition's first offset, the KafkaJS-style send reports no per-message offsets), `kafka/test-kafka.ts` `startTestKafka` (a KRaft broker with PLAINTEXT, SASL_SSL (PLAIN, SCRAM-256/512 users made by `kafka-storage format --add-scram`) and SSL with client auth listeners, advertised on 127.0.0.1; certificates are 100-year fixtures in `test/fixtures/kafka`). The server checks Kafka settings on save. 3b done: `kafka/msk-iam.ts` `mskIamToken` (`@smithy/signature-v4` 5.7.5 with a SHA-256 on node:crypto; GET `kafka.<region>.amazonaws.com/?Action=kafka-cluster:Connect` presigned for `kafka-cluster`, 900 s or less when the credentials expire sooner, `User-Agent=optiply-workflows`, base64url; `lifetime` absolute ms); `kafkaResourceClient` gives MSK IAM resources the `oauthbearer_token_refresh_cb` and names the AWS identity through STS. **Checked against AWS's own `aws-msk-iam-sasl-signer-js` 1.0.3** (a dev dependency): at the same signing moment the URLs match parameter for parameter, signature included; and the test broker's SASL listener now takes OAUTHBEARER (Kafka's unsecured validator), so a test proves librdkafka calls the raw callback through the KafkaJS-style API and connects with its token. A real MSK cluster stays unchecked (§12). 3c done (not split): `kafka/consumer.ts` `kafkaConsumer` (librdkafka's own `KafkaConsumer`: no auto-commit, `rebalance_cb: true` with the `rebalance` event to commit revoked partitions first, `consume(max, waitMs)`, `commit`/`commitNow`, pause/resume, fatal errors kept); `queue-adapters/kafka.ts` (a background poll loop so long runs never stop polling, which would drop the member after `max.poll.interval.ms`; a buffer of 25 per in-flight slot, partitions paused past it; per-key queues within a partition, keyless ones free; retries offered again after `retryBackoffSeconds·2^(n−1)` (≤ 900 s) and run as attempt n+1 through the shared `startDelivery`; after `maxAttempts` produced to the dead-letter topic with the `ow-*` headers, or dropped; a dead letter that fails is sent again later without another run; offsets committed up to the lowest unsettled every 5 s, on revoke and on stop; `reset` leaves and rejoins). `QueueMessage.input` may be a function of the attempt (Kafka's `startTrigger.message.attempt`); `backoffSecondMs` for tests. **Fixed on the way:** the Kafka client kept a disconnected producer after `close()`. `test/kafka-consumer.test.ts` (5, against the container): order per key with others alongside and the commit, retry then dead letter with headers and the key moving on, drop without a topic, handover on stop without a second run or a lost message, `maxInFlight`; passed twice. 4 done: `workflows.triggers` gives every queue trigger (Pub/Sub, SQS, Kafka) `queue: {worker, state, lastDay: {messages, succeeded, failed (incl. dead-lettered and dropped), deadLettered}}` (was `pubsub`; the web reads `queue`); releases tests on a real worker: SQS (fake AWS) and Kafka (container) messages run the live release, defaults projected (Kafka's group `ow-<workflow id>-<name>`), the editor's state seen. Releasing needing Use on trigger resources, run inputs and the triggers' state needed nothing new. 5a done: `pages/resources/queue-settings.tsx` (`AwsIdentityFields`: region, access key or this server's credentials from `resources.awsIdentity`, role and external id, locked for editors where an admin chose the server's; `SqsFields` with the endpoint for admins only; `KafkaFields`: brokers one per line, sign-in, TLS, SASL user and password, client certificate and key, CA, client id, MSK IAM's AWS fields); `lib/resource-types.ts` `MessageKind`, `MESSAGE_QUERIES`, `defaultMessageQuery`, badges SQS (red) and KF (purple); the workflow inspector's `MessageQueryForm` for all three kinds (the kind guessed from the query when its resource is gone); the playground's `MessageDraft`. Apps edit SQS and Kafka queries as JSON, like Pub/Sub's. 5b done: the triggers form's `QueueSection`/`QueueCard` for Pub/Sub, SQS and Kafka (`QUEUES` per kind; Pub/Sub's test ids kept; Kafka's topics, group, start, attempts, backoff and dead-letter topic); `lib/runs-api.ts` `queueMessageLine` (runs list and the run panel's `run-queue`; Pub/Sub's `run-pubsub` text unchanged); `scopeDeclarations(…, queues)` types `startTrigger.message` as the workflow's kinds (`OwSqsMessage`, `OwKafkaMessage`; Pub/Sub's without any). E2E setup: a fake AWS (keys in `E2E_AWS_KEY_ID`/`E2E_AWS_SECRET`, role `E2E_AWS_ROLE` with external id `optiply-e2e`, queues `orders`, `m26`, `m26-flaky` → `m26-dlq` after 2, `m26-slow` 2 s, `m26-groups.fifo`, `m26-busy`, `m26-sent`; the server's own AWS credentials are a fake user's through `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`) and, with Docker, a Kafka container (`E2E_KAFKA`, `E2E_KAFKA_SCRAM`; topics per part); control routes `/sqs/send`, `/sqs/messages`, `/kafka/produce`, `/kafka/read`; helpers `fakeSqs`, `testKafka`. `e2e/queue-triggers.spec.ts` (4: SQS resource with a key, the server's credentials offered, the playground's send; Kafka resource with SCRAM over TLS and the CA, the playground's produce; an SQS trigger and a Kafka trigger set up in the editor run per message, counted, named on the run pages) passed, with `pubsub.spec.ts`. 6a done: `apps/web/e2e/m26-done-when.spec.ts` (2 tests, a step per done-when item: SQS a run per message and its delete, a failure handed back and the redrive to the DLQ, a run outlasting the visibility, FIFO order with a group alongside, `maxInFlight`, a send query starting a trigger, an assumed role and the server's credentials, a member refused them, the secret never in a response; Kafka order per key with another key alongside, retries and the dead-letter topic with its headers while the key moves on, a produce query starting a trigger, SCRAM over TLS and mTLS, the password and key never in a response) passed first time; M19's done-when passed again (it read the triggers query's `pubsub` field, now `queue`: fixed). Full e2e 126 passed, 1 failed (`m22-done-when` under load; alone, passing). Review (code-review high): 10 found, all fixed, `a6b2214`. 6b: the image `optiply-workflows:m26-candidate` built and checked (not tagged `local`). 6c: docs (runbooks `sqs.md`, `kafka.md`, Pub/Sub's updated, `docker-compose.aws.yml`, `docker-compose.kafka.yml`, `.env.example`, §9u, §10, §12 items 33–35, §13 "From M26", architecture "As built"). **Left for the user:** rebuilding their Compose stack on M26 (ask first), then trying SQS on their AWS account (or LocalStack) and Kafka through `docker-compose.kafka.yml`.

## 9v. What M27 built (on the local branch `feat/m27-connectors`, not pushed)

The design is `architecture.md` §2.30 (with "As built"); the step record is §14 (it moves here when the next milestone is planned). The plan (`1cf0a75`) and six steps, built in away mode (the user away), each green before its commit, stacked on `feat/m26-queue-triggers`.

- **The HTTP core** (step 1a `e4158b5`): `packages/connectors/src/http-core.ts` out of `rest.ts` (REST unchanged); `Connector.httpConfig` and `httpConfigFor(type, config)`, so the server's OAuth (M9) and custom auth flows work for any HTTP type.
- **The built-in REST API resource** (step 1b `771fe9e`): `restapi.ts`, `rest_api` seeded by `migrate` (no migration), `REST_API_PRIVATE_HOSTS`, the app viewer's host rule.
- **GraphQL** (step 2 `0dd9246`): `graphql.ts`, `startTestGraphql`, `runOperation`, `resources.graphqlSchema`.
- **Services** (steps 3a `5a35ad3` Slack and the shared base, 3b `e7dac75` HubSpot, 3c `c0f4dd2` SendGrid, 3d `10ad87c` Google Sheets): `services/actions.ts` (`@ow/connectors/actions`), `services/common.ts`, a connector each, `services/fake.ts` (`startFakeServices`).
- **Server** (step 4 `2071a2a`): the services' test URLs, Sheets' server identity (its own scopes; admins only), settings checks, `resources.hubspotProperties`, types grouped, `restapi` not creatable, `ow_connector_queries_total`, `@ow/git-format` `query-text.ts` (`.graphql`, `.html`).
- **Web** (steps 5a–5b `b621551`, 5c `c96bf01`): settings forms (`service-settings.tsx`), the built-in REST API's page, the grouped picker, `components/action-query.tsx` in the inspector, the playground and the app editor; the GraphQL editor on `graphql-language-service` (`components/code/graphql.ts`); collab text keys.
- **Done-when** (step 6a `aa5696d`): `apps/web/e2e/m27-done-when.spec.ts` (2 tests) and `e2e/connectors.spec.ts` (4).
- **Ops** (step 6c): `docs/runbooks/connectors.md`, `REST_API_PRIVATE_HOSTS` in Compose and `.env.example`.
- **New dependencies:** `graphql` 16.14.2 (connectors, server, web), `graphql-language-service` 5.7.0 (web). **Migrations:** none.
- **Review fixes** (session 33, the `code-review` skill (high) on `feat/m26-queue-triggers...feat/m27-connectors`: 10 found, each checked against the code, all held, all fixed in one commit): **the built-in REST API returned its own credentials** (an API key sent as a query parameter came back in `metadata.url`, a bearer token in an echoing API's data; `rest_api` has no resource secrets for `runQuery` to scrub): it now scrubs the request's token, password, basic credentials and key (raw and URL-encoded) from results and errors; **REST's HEAD regressed** on the HTTP core (a 404 threw) and `durationMs` included the body download: both as before; Google Sheets read the header row from `Sheet1` for a bare cell range (now the first sheet's); `ow_connector_queries_total` took labels from unparsed actions (now the parsed query's); SendGrid split `"Doe, John" <…>` at the comma; the 429 wait left a listener on the signal and ignored one already aborted; the app panel kept an SQL query when switching from a missing resource; duplicates removed: `fileValue` (shared by multipart parts and service files), `apiCall` and `answer` (the services' "Call any …" and results; `..` now checked decoded, GET with a body refused), `urlEvaluate`/`renderParams` (REST and the built-in REST API).
- **Totals:** connectors 325 (283 at the end of M26), collab 85, git-format 2445 (2443), server 573 + 1 skipped (556 + 1), web unit 106; e2e: see §15.

## 9w. What M28 built (on the local branch `feat/m28-workflow-editor`, not pushed)

The design is `architecture.md` §2.31 (with "As built"); the step record is §14 (it moves here when the next milestone is planned). The plan (`9c7d621`) and eight steps, stacked on `feat/m27-connectors` (from `3706ad0`), with screenshots before and after each change (a temporary `apps/web/e2e/zz-walkthrough.spec.ts`, never committed).

- **Viewers** (step 1 `837b1df`): `editor/result-model.ts`, `editor/result-view.tsx` (`DataTab` with a virtualized table, `JsonTree`, `ResultTabs`).

Tests: 2 connector, 2 server webhook (confirmation, notifications v1 and v2, a tampered message, a foreign certificate and another topic refused). Step 4 (Kinesis): `aws/kinesis.ts` (an action type: `putRecords` in 500s, a random partition key without one; the reads the adapter needs; **HTTP/1.1** through `@smithy/node-http-handler`, since the SDK's default HTTP/2 handler is only for SubscribeToShard and fails on HTTP/1.1 servers such as the fake), `aws/fake-kinesis.ts` (shards over MD5 hash ranges, iterators, closed shards ending, splits with parent links), `triggers.kinesis` (stream name or ARN, `startAt` latest/trimHorizon, attempts, backoff), `engine/src/queue-adapters/kinesis.ts` (one record per shard at a time; checkpoints in `kinesis_checkpoints` on success and on skipping; a failed record retried before the ones after it, doubling backoff, then `dropped`; a child shard waits for its parent to be read to its end; an expired iterator made again from the checkpoint), `KinesisRunMessage`; projection defaults (latest, 3 attempts, 10 s); web: the Triggers panel's Kinesis section, `OwKinesisMessage`, the run line, `KinesisFields`. Tests: 2 connector, 3 engine (order and restart from the checkpoint, retry then skip, a split), 1 definition, 1 server (a worker runs the live release from a record and saves the checkpoint); the RabbitMQ server test's workflow renamed `on_rabbit_jobs` (it collided with the SQS test's `on_jobs` when the file runs whole). Step 5 (Schema Registry, session 37, 2026-11-04, **built, not committed yet**): `kafka/registry.ts` (Confluent's REST API through the REST client, so basic auth and the private-network guard come free; schemas by id cached, a subject's latest cached a minute; Avro via **avsc 5.7.9**, decoded to plain JSON: bytes as base64; JSON Schema as the JSON after the header; Protobuf refused), Kafka config `schemaRegistry {url, username?, allowPrivateNetwork}` and secret `schemaRegistryPassword` (settings problem: a username needs it), `kafka/fake-registry.ts`; produce: **a `subject` per message** (not the plan's query-level `subject`/`format`: the format comes from the subject's schema type, and one produce can mix subjects), Avro values checked against the schema; the trigger decodes framed values (`message.schemaId`), an undecodable one runs as it came with `message.schemaError`; web: the Kafka form's Schema Registry section, the produce hint, `OwKafkaMessage`'s fields. **A fix found on the way (M19/M26's, all brokers):** a body with a NUL byte (every wire-format value) or not UTF-8 made a run whose insert Postgres's jsonb refused, and the message was redelivered forever (Kafka: its partition's commits stalled); now `bodyData` in `pubsub.ts` gives such bytes as base64 with `message.encoding: 'base64'` (Pub/Sub, RabbitMQ, Kafka, Kinesis; SQS through it for JSON's `\u0000` escapes). Tests: 3 connector (registry), 3 engine (`body-data.test.ts`), 1 engine Kafka (a real broker: an Avro value decoded, an unknown schema as base64 with `schemaError`); the 6 consumer suites 40 passed, server connectors/resources/aws 50 passed. Committed as `6d9611a`. Step 6 (done-when and docs, session 37): `m30-done-when.spec.ts`, 2 tests: (1) SNS and Kinesis: a webhook with the `sns` preset subscribed through the fake (its confirmation fetched, no run), a Kinesis trigger set in the editor from the oldest record, one workflow publishing to the topic and putting into the stream, each delivery running its workflow, the runs page's Kinesis line; (2) with Docker: a RabbitMQ resource made, saved and tested in the UI, a Kafka resource with its Schema Registry in the UI (a username without its password refused), a RabbitMQ trigger set in the editor and a Kafka trigger from the start, one workflow publishing to the queue and producing Avro by subject, both running (the queue and its dead-letter queue empty; the Avro value decoded with its `schemaId`), the runs page's lines (the Kafka one with "· schema N"). Passed twice, alongside `queue-triggers.spec.ts` and `connectors.spec.ts` (10 passed). Docs: the three runbooks, `kafka.md`, architecture's "As built" and the M30 row ✅, §1, §9y. Committed as `03fa35e`. Step 7 (review, session 37): the `code-review` skill (high) over `54e396a..HEAD` found 10; all fixed: **Kinesis** (1) a read failing mid-round lost the records already taken and left their shards waiting for good: they're handed out; (2) `startAt: latest` read a resharded stream's children from their start: a child starts at its beginning only when it carries on from a shard this trigger read, and shards already closed are skipped; (3) a merge's `AdjacentParentShardId` was ignored: a shard waits for all its parents; (4) changing a trigger's stream (or resource, environment) reused the old shards' checkpoints: the release clears them, and the adapter's save only writes while the trigger row still names its stream (`FOR SHARE`, so it waits for such a release); **RabbitMQ** (5) the host's private-address check could be passed by DNS rebinding: `guardedLookup` on the socket too; (6) the default hold (60 min) outlasted RabbitMQ's `consumer_timeout` (30): 25 min for RabbitMQ, the field's hint says why; (7) no `undelivered`: a delivery whose run couldn't start kept a prefetch slot: requeued after 5 s; and `storable` in the consumer turns a NUL in any run input (a header) into U+FFFD, so such a run starts at all; **Kafka** (8) a decode failure stuck to a message's retries: decoded again; **SNS** (9) unauthenticated deliveries could make the server fetch a certificate URL each: in-flight fetches shared, failures remembered 5 min, at most 10 new certificates a minute; (10) the queue kinds repeated as literals: `QUEUE_TRIGGER_KINDS` in `@ow/definition` (the engine's SQL, validate, releases, the web's editor). Tests: 3 engine Kinesis (fake: `merge`, `failNext`), 2 engine RabbitMQ, 1 engine Kafka (fake registry: `down`), `storable`'s, 1 server SNS, the Kinesis release test extended; each new test failed on the code before its fix. Runbooks updated (RabbitMQ's hold and timeout, Kinesis's `latest` and stream changes, SNS's certificate limit). Checks: biome ci; typecheck of every package; definition 193, connectors 380, engine 212, web 125, server 583 (+1 skipped) passed; **full e2e 145 passed, 1 failed**: `webhook-settings.spec.ts`'s body-schema test, a flake from before M30 (on the step 6 build it failed 4 of 5 alone): its `.suggest-widget` locator met the schema editor's hidden widget too (strict mode), or Monaco's worker was slow under the full run; now `.suggest-widget.visible`, 6 of 6 passed.

## 9aa. What M32 built (on `feat/m32-python-packages`)

The design is `architecture.md` §2.35 (with "As built"); the step record is below. Stacked on `feat/m31-git-hosts` (`5752365`). Built in away mode (session 38).

Env jails start only when the base pool is full and count towards `PYTHON_WARM_START_PARALLEL`. A root's failed import isn't logged (the jail's stderr is the code's): the jail comes up without it. Step 2 (indexes in `builder.py`: `check_indexes`, credentials through pip's environment (`PIP_INDEX_URL`, `PIP_EXTRA_INDEX_URL`, percent-encoded; **not netrc** as planned: netrc can't hold spaces and keys by host), `attribute` naming each package's index from the extra indexes' pages (by its hash), `/install` in **one** pip call with every index the lock names (the hashes pin each file), `probe` for `/check-index` and to explain a failed build, `hide` for passwords echoed; `PYTHON_ALLOW_INSECURE_INDEXES`; the test index's basic auth; `test/builder-indexes.test.ts`, 7 tests; executor suite 58). Step 3 (sdists: `sdist-jail.cfg`; the builder's image gains `build-essential` and nsjail; nsjail runs **as the builder's user** (a user namespace; no cgroups, which need root: `rlimit_as` `PYTHON_SDIST_MEMORY_MB`, `rlimit_nproc` in the jail's own namespace, the container's 2g); `resolve` loops: pip's "Could not find a version that satisfies the requirement X" → `find_sdist` on the indexes pip searched (pip's vendored `packaging`) → `fetch` (the sign-in only to the index's own host, hash checked) → `build_requirements` (pyproject read from the archive) → `pip download --only-binary` into a wheelhouse → `jail_build` (`pip wheel --no-index`) → `envs/.wheels/<sha>/`, then pip again with `--find-links`; `/install` uses the kept wheel or builds it again (`locked_sdist_wheel`); the cleaner removes kept wheels unused for `PYTHON_ENV_KEEP_DAYS`; test sdists carry their own backend (`buildSdist`, a ustar writer); `test/builder-sdists.test.ts`, 6 tests, the jail probe telling no network by `ENETUNREACH`; executor suite 64. Step 4 (server: migration **0037** `python_indexes` (slug, name, url, username, `password_config_var` (a config var's **name**, as webhook signatures do), mode) and `python_envs.index_id`; `requirementsHash(requirements, indexId)` keeps M23's keys without an index; `settings.python.index` (definition, `invalid_python_index`; Git: `# index: <slug>` as `requirements.txt`'s first line, since the format keeps `settings.python` out of the JSON); `python-envs.ts`: `pythonIndexBySlug`, `builderIndex` (the password from the default environment), `lockIndexes`; the `pythonIndexes` router (list for everyone without credentials' details, admin create/update (slug fixed; envs of the index dropped)/delete (refused while a draft or live release names it)/test); `workflows.pythonEnv` takes the index and answers `details`; an unknown index fails installs and publishing as `python_packages` (not a save-time problem: the definition can't see the database); `test/python-indexes.test.ts` 4 tests; the package tests' Python blocks get a 60 s timeout and 120 s per test: under the full suite's load the 10 s block timeout (which counts the wait for packages) ran out; full server suite 605 + 1 skipped). Step 5 (web: Settings → Python (`pages/settings/python.tsx`: the indexes, New index/Edit with Test, Delete; admins only; the slug from the name, fixed once made); the workflow's Python packages field gains "From" (the index select, kept when the lines change; an index not on this instance listed as such) and the installed packages' index and "built from source"; Install now installs what the field holds **now** (the done-when caught it installing the 600 ms-settled value, without a just-picked index); shots in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-optiply-optiply-workflows/b877d319-03d1-41e7-99bf-81415c05d4bf/scratchpad/m32-shots/before` and `/after`; web unit tests 126). Step 7 (the review, `code-review` high: 10 found, all fixed: **a deadlock** between the base pool's and the env pools' maintenance threads (each read the other's counts under its own lock; now read before taking it); **an index's sign-in following a redirect to another host** (now an unredirected header, kept by `SignedRedirects` only on the index's host, never https→http); attribution guessing when an index's page failed (now matched by the download URL's prefix, else pages read strictly: a failure fails the build); relative sdist links resolved against the requested URL, not the one served; idle env jails' memory (`PYTHON_WARM_ENV_MAX_MB`, 256: a heavier one isn't kept, `tooBig` in `/health`); a wheel built from an sdist not checked against its project and version (`check_built`; pip's own `wheel` refuses it first in the test); a 404 index root taken for a good sign-in (Test now fails it, saying GitLab answers 404 to a wrong sign-in; a failed build doesn't blame it); a broken kept-wheel folder crashing the build (replaced now); Install now hiding an unrecorded failure behind a stale "Installed"; attribution's page per package (now only when the URL doesn't tell). ## 10. How things were verified (and how to repeat it)

- **Unit and integration tests:** `pnpm test`. Each server, db, connector and engine Postgres test file boots its own embedded Postgres. The engine suite was run three times in a row after step 3 with no flakes.
- **Webhook latency:** `pnpm --filter @ow/server bench:webhooks` (prints p50/p90/p99).
- **Sandbox context speed:** `pnpm --filter @ow/sandbox bench` (0.66 ms locally).
- **Load:** at load averages of 10–30 (all 13 suites at once while macOS indexes, Chrome, VS Code and Slack run) timing tests fail that pass alone: sandbox timers, code-executor speed and limits, an engine loop batch, m9's scheduled run, app-sandbox's restart. Rerun the package alone (`pnpm turbo run test --filter <pkg> --concurrency=1`) before suspecting code; one such failure was a real bug (`b06f4ff`). Turbo stopping a suite midway can leave `mysql:8.4` and `ow-code-executor-test-*` containers running: remove them by name.
- **Playwright e2e (committed):** `pnpm --filter @ow/web build && pnpm --filter @ow/server build && pnpm --filter @ow/web e2e`. Locally it uses the installed Chrome (`channel: 'chrome'`); CI uses Playwright's Chromium. The server runs on port 4387 (`E2E_PORT`). Fifteen specs at the end of M8 (nine after M7); 27 tests in 20 files with M12's Python spec (it needs the executor container: Docker). For repeats, pass `--repeat-each=3` to `pnpm exec playwright test` in `apps/web` (through `pnpm … e2e -- …` the flag doesn't reach Playwright). The `typeInto` helper clicks a Monaco field near its top-left corner: with more lines than fit, the middle of `.view-lines` is over the next field.
- **MySQL tests** start a `mysql:8.4` container (Docker) or use `MYSQL_TEST_URL`; skipped locally without Docker, required in CI (~46 s for the connector file, including the container start).
- **Docker Compose, session 22, later** (the M19 done-when's last part, by hand: a real topic and subscription):
  - The user said go ahead and made the topic `m19-check` and subscription `m19-check-sub` in `optiply-workflows` (`gcloud services enable pubsub.googleapis.com` first; no billing prompt). The M18 image tagged `m18-backup`; the M19 image built from a clean worktree at `08c4cde`; `docker compose up -d --no-build api worker` ran `migrate` (`0023`, `0024`) first, back after 10 s.
  - The user did it in the browser: a Pub/Sub resource on the server's identity, a workflow with a trigger and a code block, published, then `gcloud pubsub topics publish m19-check --message '{"hello": "from gcloud"}' --attribute kind=check`. The run succeeded with that data and attribute; the delivery record said acked after one run; no Pub/Sub warnings.
  - To repeat: a topic and a pull subscription, a Pub/Sub resource, a workflow with a trigger on it (published), a `gcloud pubsub topics publish`.

  - `docker compose up -d --build` with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000`; admin via `docker compose exec api node dist/cli/create-admin.js`.

  - The `orders` workflow (webhook → query → JS → branch → two Responses), published v1.0.0: `curl` with `X-Api-Key` returned the production answer, `?environment=staging` (through the alias `customers/:customerId/orders`) the staging one; no key → 401; `Prefer: respond-async` → 202 + `Location`; the same `Idempotency-Key` twice → the same run, replayed.
  - **`kill -9` of a worker mid-run:** a live run of `a (3 s) → b (25 s) → c` on a worker; while `b` ran, `docker kill -s KILL` on the container holding the lease. The other worker resumed it once the 30 s lease expired; the run succeeded with 1 recovery and epoch 3; `a` ran once (not re-run), `b` re-ran (its first insert rolled back with the killed connection). Note: `docker kill` counts as a manual stop, so `restart: unless-stopped` doesn't restart the container; `docker compose up -d worker` does.
  - **1-minute cron, 2-minute run:** the `slow-cron` workflow (`insert` a hit, then `select pg_sleep(120)`, block timeout 150 s) with schedule `* * * * *`, overlap `skip`, watched for 15 minutes through the database: runs fired at 09:01, 09:04, 09:07, 09:10 and 09:13 (each 120 s; the fire at the minute a run ends is checked just before it finishes, so one starts every 3 minutes), 10 fires were logged as skipped, and a self-join on the runs' start and finish times found **0 overlapping pairs**. The first three runs failed with "statement timeout": a Postgres resource's `statementTimeoutMs` defaults to 120 s and caps the block timeout (M1); after raising it to 180 s, the next two succeeded. Then v1.0.1 turned the schedule off (its trigger kept its id, `next_fire_at` null).
- **Bundled server without Docker:** `node --no-node-snapshot dist/main.js` with `MIGRATIONS_DIR=../../packages/db/migrations` (and `WEB_DIST_DIR=../web/dist` to serve the UI). Restart it after rebuilding the web app: `@fastify/static` lists files once at startup, and stale asset names return the SPA's HTML (a MIME-type error in the browser).
- **Browser walkthroughs** (sessions 3–6, not committed): session 3 used puppeteer-core scripts; sessions 4–6 used a temporary Playwright spec (`e2e/zz-walkthrough.spec.ts`, deleted afterwards) reusing `e2e/global-setup.ts` with screenshots read back (session 6: the MySQL and BigQuery forms, the Inbox, the task form, the User Task form and the audit page). Gotchas:
  - Typing into Monaco races its auto-closing on slow machines: use `keyboard.insertText` (see `e2e/helpers.ts`).
  - Monaco wraps long lines on screen: rendered text contains extra line breaks.
  - Click empty canvas away from the floating toolbar (top left), controls (bottom left) and minimap (bottom right).
  - "Not published" also matches a button named "Publish": use `exact: true`.
  - A field label containing "host" (e.g. the SSL option "…hostname") matches `getByLabel('Host')`: prefer `getByRole('textbox', { name })`. Fields inside a `Field` `<label>` take its hint into their accessible name; buttons inside one take the whole label (the label editors' "Add").

## 11. Local environment quirks (this Mac)

- **Optiply's own API as a REST resource** (tried by the user in session 18): use the base URL **`https://api.optiply.com/v1/`** with the trailing slash. Without it, `GET /v1` answers 302 to an internal hostname (`http://public-api-node-2.public-api.svc.cluster.local/v1/`, from its Istio gateway), which the resource rightly refuses (Test connection fails `blocked`). Queries with a path aren't affected. Worth reporting to Optiply's API team. The API is JSON:API: send `Accept: application/vnd.api+json`; a 404 "Resource not found." is a record the token can't see.
- **The Compose stack** runs M17 (session 20: `fc35ec1` with `9840ec7`'s change, built from a clean worktree), which includes M16's review fixes and `41b7408`'s. It was built from a clean worktree (`docker build -t optiply-workflows:local .` there, then `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose up -d`) so uncommitted files in the tree stay out. Older images: `optiply-workflows:m15-backup` (M15) and `m14-backup` (M14); `m16-check` (session 19) is kept as the user asked. A second API process for checks needs its own `PUBLIC_URL` (the collab socket checks the Origin).
- Node v24.15.0 (arm64). **Docker Desktop 29.8 with Compose v5.6 is installed** (since session 4; 8 CPUs, about 4 GB for Docker). No global pnpm, no system Postgres.
- Run pnpm as **`COREPACK_ENABLE_DOWNLOAD_PROMPT=0 corepack pnpm …`**. **Turbo needs a `pnpm` binary on PATH.** In a Claude session, run `corepack enable --install-directory <scratchpad>/bin pnpm` and prepend that directory to PATH. The user can simply run `corepack enable`.
- **Dev database:** `pnpm dev:db` stores its data in `.data/pg` (gitignored), with `DATABASE_URL=postgres://ow:ow@127.0.0.1:5433/optiply_workflows`. It has a `demo_customers` table, a "Demo DB" resource (`demo_db`), and test workflows from session 3, all safe to delete; it's still at migration `0003` and migrates on the next `pnpm dev`.
- **Compose stack, end of session 14:** the image of `feat/optiply-ui` at `c00c6d8` (step 6, **before the review fixes**: no minimap button, chart and code colours still hex copies); `api` and `worker` recreated with `--no-deps`, Postgres and its data untouched. The same database as below. To bring it to `main`: `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose build api && PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose up -d --no-deps api worker` (check the user isn't using it first).
- **Compose stack, end of session 13:** the image of `feat/m13-editor-polish` at `767222b` (M13 steps 1–4; rebuilt for the Compose check, §10). The user's `test` workflow and a resource of theirs on it; `m13-check@optiply.nl` disabled.
- **Compose stack: Docker Desktop was reset to factory in session 13** (after an error the user couldn't capture). That deleted every image and the `optiply-workflows_pgdata` volume, and there was no backup, so everything the history below describes is gone (the `admin@optiply.nl` and `m7-check` admins, the user's `test` workflow, API key and `cancel-me` schedule, the managed database's contents). It was rebuilt from `main` at `13ea53e` with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose up --build -d`: migrations from scratch, the managed database re-provisioned (`owdb_production`, `owdb_staging`), the code executor, Google SSO on. Its only user is **`fabioceriaco@optiply.nl`** (admin as the first user, signed in with Google; also has a local password). **Always start or recreate it with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000`**: Compose reads `.env`, whose `PUBLIC_URL` is Vite's `:5173`, and the Google callback is built from it. Docker Desktop's memory is the factory 4 GB.
- **The Compose stack before the reset** (volume `optiply-workflows_pgdata`): it ran the image of **`feat/m12-python`** at `ec7280b` (rebuilt in session 12 with the user's OK), **with the `code-executor` service** and **`PYTHON_EXECUTOR_TOKEN` in `.env`** (added in session 12; every `docker compose` command, even `ps`, refuses to run without it). Python works on it. Session 12's check left nothing behind (§10); `m7-check`'s password is in session 12's scratchpad. Before that: the image of **`feat/m11-git-sync`** (step 6, uncommitted at build time but the same code as the step 6 commit; rebuilt in session 11 with the user's OK; **migrations 0014–0016 applied**, so going back to `main`'s image runs it on a newer schema: additive tables and columns only). Git sync isn't set up on it. Session 11's check left nothing behind (§10); `m7-check`'s password is in session 11's scratchpad. Before that: the image of `main` at `bb7f6b7` (M10 with its review fixes; migration `0013`). Session 10's check left nothing behind (§10); `m7-check`'s password is in session 10's scratchpad. Before that: the image of `main` at `a04c68e` (M9 with the PR #11 review fixes; no OAuth connections stored). Before that: the M9 step 7 image (session 9's Compose check). Before that: the image of `main` at `fe0c65d` (M8 with the PR #9 review fixes; migration `0012`). It holds no apps (the user made and deleted a `test` app in session 8). Before that: the M7 step 7 image (session 7), with the managed database provisioned (`owdb_production`, `owdb_staging`; no tables). Also holds an admin `m7-check@optiply.nl` used by the session 7 and 8 checks (its password was reset again in session 9, kept in that session's scratchpad at `compose/m7-check-password`; reset it again with `create-admin` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD`). Before that: the M6 step 7 image. Holds session 5's `shop` database and resource, the draft workflows `loop-10k`, `wait-restart` and `cancel-me`, and **the user's own work from session 6** (a `test` workflow, an API key, and a schedule on `cancel-me` that fires: check with the user before changing them). Admin `admin@optiply.nl`: the password was reset in session 6 to a random one kept in that session's scratchpad (`compose/admin-password`); set your own with `docker compose exec api node --no-node-snapshot dist/cli/create-admin.js --email admin@optiply.nl --name Admin`. Compose reads `.env`, whose `PUBLIC_URL` points at Vite (`:5173`): run it with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000` (or change `.env`). It holds port 3000, like `pnpm dev`. Postgres isn't published to the host: use `docker compose exec postgres psql -U ow -d optiply_workflows`.
- **Don't kill side processes inside the Postgres container** (see the runbook): Postgres is PID 1 there and crash-restarts when an adopted child dies by signal.
- **The session scratchpad can be cleared mid-session** (it happened in session 6): recreate the `pnpm` shim with `corepack enable --install-directory <scratchpad>/bin pnpm`.
- **isolated-vm** loads its prebuilt addon since 7.0.2 (session 40; it compiled from the patched source before, needing the Xcode command-line tools).
- **Docker images used by tests:** `mysql:8.4` (pulled on first use) and, since M12, `ow-code-executor:test-<hash>` (built from `apps/code-executor` by the first test that needs it: ~35 s with the layers cached, a few minutes cold; old tags can be removed with `docker image rm`).
- **`.env`** (gitignored) has `SESSION_SECRET`, `LOCAL_LOGIN_ENABLED=true`, a generated `ENCRYPTION_KEYS=v1:…`, `PYTHON_EXECUTOR_TOKEN` and, since session 13, `OIDC_CLIENT_ID` and `OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET`: an OAuth client "optiply-workflows local" (Web application) in the user's Google Cloud project `optiply-workflows`, consent screen **Internal**, redirect URIs `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/oidc/callback` and `http://localhost:5173/api/auth/oidc/callback`. The user owns the project; `gcloud`'s default project on this Mac is another one (`clear-veld-186016`) and its login had expired in session 13. `WORKER_CONCURRENCY` defaults to 4 and `WEBHOOK_INLINE_CONCURRENCY` to 8 when unset.
- **Dev admins (dev database):** `admin@optiply.nl` (password given to the user in session 2) and `m2-smoke@optiply.nl` (session 3, random password; harmless). Set a password with `corepack pnpm --filter @ow/server create-admin -- --email admin@optiply.nl --name Admin` (prompts, or reads `ADMIN_PASSWORD`).
- **At the end of session 13** the Compose stack was left running on `main`'s image (`13ea53e`) with an empty database, the code executor and Google SSO (above). The dev database and dev servers weren't started.
- **At the end of session 12** the Compose stack was left running on the M12 branch's image with the code executor (above). The dev database and dev servers weren't started. The e2e builds are from step 6. The executor test image (`ow-code-executor:test-<hash>`) is built by the first test needing it; older tags are removed when a new one is built. `pnpm dev` has no executor: run one with `docker run -d --rm --cap-add SYS_ADMIN --security-opt seccomp=apps/code-executor/seccomp.json --security-opt apparmor=unconfined -e PYTHON_EXECUTOR_TOKEN=… -p 127.0.0.1:8080:8080 optiply-workflows-code-executor:local` and set `PYTHON_EXECUTOR_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080` and the token in `.env`.

## 12. Still unverified

1. ~~**Google SSO** against real Google.~~ **Checked in session 13** (2026-10-17) on the Compose stack: `/api/auth/oidc/start` redirected to Google with `redirect_uri=http://localhost:3000/api/auth/oidc/callback`, `hd=optiply.nl`, PKCE (S256), state and nonce; the user signed in with Google and was created as the first user, in Admins, with the Google subject stored. Not tried: a non-optiply.nl account (the Internal consent screen refuses it at Google; the server's domain check is the backstop) and an HTTPS `PUBLIC_URL`.
2. **BigQuery against a real project**: needs a service account (BigQuery Job User + Data Viewer) and a dataset. Only the token endpoint was checked live.
3. **MySQL `verify-full` TLS** against a server with a certificate from a real or private CA (tested: `require` works, a self-signed certificate is rejected).
4. **Provisioning on managed Postgres** (Cloud SQL, RDS) with a non-superuser `CREATEROLE` user: tested on embedded Postgres 18 only. Cloud SQL's `cloudsqlsuperuser` and RDS's `rds_superuser` aren't real superusers.
5. **A restore onto a different Postgres server** (the roles missing): reasoned from the dump and covered by the provisioning test that drops the role; not run end to end.
6. **The 50 MB / 500k-row import caps**: enforced in code, not exercised (the largest import tested was 50,000 rows).
7. **Prometheus scraping** by a real Prometheus (the endpoints were scraped with curl and fetch only); no dashboards or alerts exist yet.
8. **Apps in Firefox and Safari**: the Worker sandbox's isolation was spiked in Firefox 157 and WebKit 27.2 (Playwright's builds, session 10: everything refused, as in Chrome); the rest (the running app, the watchdog, react-grid-layout, the Table, pages and Back/Forward) was exercised in Chrome only (locally and Chromium in CI).
9. **OAuth against real providers** (Google, HubSpot, Slack, Microsoft 365): the presets and quirks were checked against the providers' documentation and a fake provider only. Needs an OAuth app per provider with the redirect URI `<PUBLIC_URL>/api/oauth/callback` and its client ID and secret from the user. **Slack requires HTTPS redirect URIs** (as far as I know): its live check needs a tunnel or an HTTPS `PUBLIC_URL`.
10. **The OAuth popup in Firefox and Safari** (`window.open` plus `postMessage` from the result page; checked in Chrome only).
11. **Large app data**: the Table virtualizes rows and pages server-side (100k rows in the done-when, 25 at a time), but every query result still crosses the sandbox and the store whole: a big unpaged result (thousands of rows in `scroll` mode) wasn't measured.
12. **Git sync against real GitHub** (session 11 used a local git server and a fake GitHub; **session 23 checked part of it**: pushes over https with a fine-grained token through askpass, the pull request API, deploys from GitHub's merge commits, a merge commit making a conflicting PR mergeable; still not seen: what follows): pushing over https with a fine-grained token through askpass, the REST pull request calls, GitHub's push webhook and its signature, branch protection refusing pushes to main (`[remote rejected]`), fetching a commit by sha (`ensureCommit`), and GitHub Enterprise Server's `/api/v3`. Needs a scratch repo and a fine-grained token (Contents and Pull requests, read and write, on that repo only) from the user.
13. **Big repos**: every deploy of a new commit reads and decodes every directory under `workflows/` and `apps/` (fine for tens of objects; not measured for hundreds), and the bare cache fetches whole branches.
14. **The Python executor outside Docker Compose** (Kubernetes, another container runtime): it needs `SYS_ADMIN`, the seccomp profile, AppArmor off and cgroup v2 (`docs/runbooks/python.md`); checked on Docker Desktop (arm64) and GitHub's Ubuntu runners (x86-64, AppArmor) only.
15. **Many Python runs at once**: the executor's concurrency (4 in Compose) and memory (2 GB) were exercised with 8 runs at a time in tests, not under a real load.
16. **A Run lost right after reopening the app editor** (M16, session 20): twice in about 80 runs of the done-when, the app's final Run (just after the editor reopened) left the result `null`, both times right after the workflow story; not reproduced with probes in about 40 more runs, nor single-user (0 of 15). Not the code panel remounting (`epoch` only moves on undo/redo). The spec retries that click; the cause is unknown.
17. **The app editor in dev under React StrictMode** with the shared document (M16): reasoned about (the session reopens on the remount), not run: the dev server's API needs :3000, which the Compose stack holds (§11).
18. **Multiplayer with many people or across real networks** (M16): tested with two (and a watcher) on one machine and two API processes; not with ten people, high latency or a real dropped Wi-Fi (the outage was simulated by closing the socket; a dead connection without a close is found by the server's 30 s ping).
19. **SSH tunnels to Optiply's real servers** (M17): checked against OpenSSH 9.2 (Debian bookworm) in Docker with ed25519 host keys only; not against Optiply's own bastion, other SSH servers (Dropbear, Windows OpenSSH, cloud bastion services), RSA or ECDSA host keys, or real network latency.
20. **Many tunnelled resources at once** (M17): one SSH connection per resource configuration and process (the API and each worker); not measured with tens of resources or under load (SSH servers' `MaxStartups`/`MaxSessions` may matter).
21. **IAP against real Google** (M18): the protocol was written from the Cloud SDK's source (`iap_tunnel_websocket*.py`) and tested in depth against our fake endpoint only. **Checked in session 22** against Optiply's bastion: connect, data both ways and acks (an SSH session and Postgres queries), with the server's identity from an `authorized_user` file. **Still unverified against real Google:** resume after a real drop (and IAP's real reconnect timing), the close codes other than a clean close (4003, 4004, 4033, 4047 come from the SDK and IAP's error reports), a service account key's token (only BigQuery's token endpoint was checked live, in M1), and the metadata server on a GCE VM.
22. **One full connector-suite run failed once** (session 21, M18 step 3b; which test is unknown, the output wasn't kept); 30 runs since passed.
23. **Pub/Sub against real Google** (M19): checked once on Compose: a message pulled, run, acked, with the server's identity (an `authorized_user` file). **Not seen against real Pub/Sub:** a nack and its redelivery with the subscription's retry policy, a dead-letter topic, deadline extensions over a long run, ordering keys, a service account key's token for Pub/Sub, `deliveryAttempt`, and any load (many messages, many triggers, one worker holding them all).
24. **Merges of large definitions** (M20): diff3 merges 10,000 lines in 90 ms, but whole apps with hundreds of components merging with many conflicts weren't timed, nor the merge dialog with dozens of conflicts.
25. **Nested modules at scale** (M21): three levels with two instances each were run (Playwright and the runtime tests); not pages with many nested instances (each is its own runtime in the Worker), nor the nesting check over hundreds of module releases (it loads every release's components per check).
26. **Nested modules on Compose** (M21): the user's by-hand check covered library queries in workflows and the multiplayer library editor; whether they also built modules three deep there wasn't said (Playwright covers it).
27. **A flake in M19's done-when** (seen once, session 24, in the full e2e run after M21's review fixes: 82 passed, 1 failed): `m19-done-when.spec.ts` line 281 expected no delivery on `paused-sub` after its trigger was turned off, and saw 1 (likely a pull already in flight when the trigger stopped). Three fresh runs of the spec alone passed; the earlier full runs that day (after 5b, 6b, 7a) passed it too. Not M21's code; a fix would wait for the in-flight pull to settle before asserting, or have stopping a trigger nack what it pulled. **Seen three times more in session 29** (the full runs after M24's 2c, 3a-2 and 3b, the same step each time; passed alone; M24 doesn't touch Pub/Sub). It now fails in every full run: worth fixing soon (the spec waiting for an in-flight pull to settle, or a stopped trigger nacking what it pulled). **Fixed in session 29 (between M24's 3b and 4a):** the cause was the **fake**, not the subscriber: `startFakePubSub` held a pull open waiting for a message and, when one was published, "delivered" it to a request whose client had already aborted (the subscriber aborts its in-flight pull when its trigger stops), counting a delivery real Pub/Sub never makes. The fake now notices the caller hanging up (`res` `close` before the answer), wakes the waiting pull and takes nothing. `pubsub-client.test.ts` has the case (it fails against the old fake: 1 delivery). `--repeat-each` on the M19 spec fails by design (run 1's live triggers keep pulling the shared subscriptions); judge it in a full run.
28. **Settings in Git across real instances** (M22): two instances on one repo were run in Playwright (a fake GitHub) and one instance on Compose against a scratch repo; not two real instances deploying different branches with Promote between them, nor resources with OAuth accounts or SSH/IAP settings arriving from Git on another instance (a consent change asking to reconnect is tested on the server only).
29. **The 10-minute retry timer** (M22, `GIT_RETRY_MINUTES`): exercised with short timers in the server tests; on Compose only the retry after a change (the audit mark) was seen.
30. **Python packages on x86-64** (M23): the builder, the locks and the envs ran on arm64 only (the Mac's Docker); a lock moved between platforms is resolved again with its versions pinned (tested with a made-up platform), not tried between real ones. A private index with credentials wasn't tried (credentials in the URL are scrubbed from logs, untested end to end).
31. **The warm pool under load** (M23): measured one caller at a time; not several workers' runs at once with the pool emptying, nor `PYTHON_WARM_MAX_IDLE_SECONDS` replacing idle jails over hours (the replacement is exercised only by its code path at start).
32. **A flake in M22's `git-health.spec.ts`** (seen once, session 28, in the full e2e after M23's second review pass: 95 passed): after Deploy now and a reload the admin banner wasn't there within the default wait (line 58); the spec passed alone twice right after, and in the full run before. Nothing M23 changed touches Git sync; a full run shares one e2e repo, so the deploy may simply have been slower. Watch for it again. **Seen again in session 29** (M24 step 2a's second full run, 97 passed; a `toBeVisible` wait), passing alone twice right after; the spec doesn't use the `typeInto` helper that step changed. Twice now: worth a longer wait or a look at the deploy's timing when there's room.

33. **SQS against real AWS** (M26): the fake SQS and STS check signatures, visibility, redrive and FIFO groups as AWS documents them; real SQS, real IAM policies and the server's own credentials (an instance role, SSO) are left for the Compose check (the user's account).
34. **MSK IAM against a real MSK cluster** (M26): the token equals AWS's own signer's, and librdkafka's OAUTHBEARER callback is proven against Kafka's unsecured validator; a real MSK cluster (`kafka-cluster:*` policies) wasn't to hand.
35. **Kafka at scale** (M26): one partition per test topic; not many partitions with rebalances while runs are under way, outside consumers sharing a group, or a lagging group of millions of messages (the buffer pauses partitions; its memory with large values wasn't measured).

36. **Slack, HubSpot and SendGrid against the real services** (M27): checked against fakes written from their API documentation (Slack's `ok: false` errors, cursors and two-step upload; HubSpot's CRM v3 search, batch upsert, v4 associations and error bodies; SendGrid's Mail Send payload and EU host); a real workspace, portal and account are the Compose check's. HubSpot's OAuth token endpoint and Slack's OAuth were exercised only against fakes (Slack's up to the redirect to its consent screen).
37. **Google Sheets against the real API** (M27): the fake follows the Sheets v4 documentation (A1 ranges, trailing empty cells left out, `USER_ENTERED`); not checked: real A1 edge cases (merged cells, named ranges, very wide sheets), Drive sharing, and the server's identity with gcloud's application-default login (which must be asked for the Sheets scopes).
38. **GraphQL against real servers** (M27): checked against graphql-js; servers that answer errors with non-200 statuses differently, persisted queries, or huge schemas (introspection size, editor speed) aren't.
39. **The built-in REST API's guard at scale** (M27): private addresses refused by literal check and at connect time (DNS), as REST's; not checked against DNS rebinding beyond what the connect-time check covers.
40. **A flake in M11's `m11-done-when.spec.ts`** (seen session 35, in a 13-spec run and twice in a repeat right after; it passed in that day's full e2e and alone after): the app's Commit dialog said "Nothing to commit" after the query edit showed `Saved`, so the commit was opened before the branch draft's save reached the server (the spec waits for a `Saved` that may be the earlier one). Not M28 code (the app editor). A fix: wait for the save after the edit (`save-status` changing), or reopen the dialog until the file is listed.
41. **M32 against real indexes and PyPI's sdists** (session 38): package indexes were checked against the local test index (basic auth, 401/403, redirects to another host) and sdists against test sdists with their own build backend; not against Artifactory, CodeArtifact, devpi or GitLab's registry (their roots, redirects and 404s), nor a real sdist needing a compiler (numpy-style builds, `build-essential` is in the image). On Compose: `docker compose build python-builder code-executor`, migration 0037, then a workflow requiring a PyPI package that has only an sdist.
42. **Timing tests under load** (session 38): `executor.test.ts`'s "counts a warm jail's limits from its request" (1.5 s of CPU in a 2 s timeout) failed once in a full executor run with the sdist builds beside it, and M23's done-when once beside M32's; both passed alone and in later full runs.
43. **M33 against real registries and machines** (session 38): tokens, the CLI and npm registries were checked against the test server and a fake registry (Bearer tokens, `/-/whoami`); not against GitHub Packages, GitLab's npm registry, Artifactory or Verdaccio (their paths and whoami), nor the CLI on Windows (`fs.watch` recursive, paths). `webhook-settings.spec.ts`'s completions test failed in two loaded full e2e runs (sessions 38) and passed alone each time.
44. **M35 against real providers** (session 39): both AI connectors were checked against the fakes only (`startFakeAi`: Anthropic's Messages API shapes from SDK 1.3.0's types, an OpenAI-compatible server). Not yet: the user's Anthropic key on their stack (Test, an extract, an agent), the refusal fallback beta (`server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`) against the real API, OpenAI's `max_completion_tokens` and non-strict structured output against api.openai.com, Ollama/vLLM function calling.

## 13. Known gaps and TODOs carried forward

Rewritten in session 40 (2026-11-05) to what's still true on `feat/m35-ai-actions`: entries fixed by later milestones, "not pushed" and "not reviewed" notes are gone (every branch M15–M35 is pushed and was reviewed; M15–M26 are on `main` through PR #22, M27–M35 go in the one PR at the end). Fix candidates were marked ★ (all fixed in session 40, below).

### Fix candidates ★

All eight fixed in session 40 (2026-11-05), one commit each on `feat/m35-ai-actions`: `14e5093` isolated-vm 7.0.2 (patch dropped), `5df07f8` the `function_call` message, `70fed30` the library editor's default query per resource type, `139addc` secret config values scrubbed from block data in the runs API, `c1d69da` idempotency keys per environment (**migration 0040**), `0c5c417` body schema patterns on RE2 (**new native dependency `re2`**), `c56f90d` OAuth revocation on Disconnect and custom-flow sign-in back-off, `5329975` Connect for `auth_required` in the workflow editor. What each leaves open is noted in its area below.

### Engine and runs
- **Sandbox isolation inside a run:** when the watchdog disposes a run's isolate (a code block stuck in an async loop), other code blocks evaluating at that moment in the same run fail with "Sandbox was reset". Retries absorb it.
- **Retry backoff sleeps in the worker**, holding a slot. Long backoffs could requeue the run with `availableAt` instead.
- `pending` block status is never written.
- **Secret config values in run data** (session 40): the runs API scrubs block data and stored replies with the environment's secret values **as they are now** (`secretConfigValues`), so an old value of a rotated secret shows in older runs; the database keeps the data as the blocks returned it (resumed and parent runs read it back); the reply a webhook caller gets isn't scrubbed (the workflow chose to send it).
- **Paused runs resume on the workers' 5 s poll** (like schedules), so up to 5 s late; children wake their parent at once.
- **No per-run cap on in-flight `fetch` requests** (the architecture's 50); the shared agent allows 32 connections per origin.
- **A loop's sync workflow runner holds the worker** while its children run (within the iteration timeout, default 10 s); a Workflow block pauses instead.
- **Renaming a function parameter doesn't rewrite `params.x`** in its blocks (renaming functions and blocks does). Functions can't be test-run on their own (their params' `testValue` is stored but unused).
- A webhook run executing in the API process is resumed by a worker if the API dies (lease); the caller's sync request just fails (expected; for ops docs).
- Workflow code has no `setTimeout` (by design); slow steps use a Wait block.
- **Scheduler:** fires up to 5 s late; a skipped fire (overlap `skip`) is only logged, not recorded as a run; the catch-up after very long downtime of a dense cron steps through at most 10k fire times.
- **Migration 0040** (session 40: idempotency keys per workflow and environment) drops and recreates `workflow_runs_idempotency_uq` without `CONCURRENTLY`, like 0008: fine at the user's size; a large production table needs it by hand.
- **Retention:** runs every 24 h from the first check, not at a fixed hour; migration 0008's `CREATE INDEX` isn't concurrent (a large production table needs `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` by hand).
- **Metrics:** the database gauges keep their last values if a read fails; no histogram of queue wait per run, no per-connector query metrics.
- **Audit export** stops at 100,000 rows.
- **Unexplained:** one session-5 run of the crash test hung for 15 minutes; not seen again.

### Webhooks (M4, M15)
- No `?api_key=` query parameter; API keys can read single runs, not list them, and only start webhook runs (which have no `current_user`).
- Rate limits are fixed windows (up to twice the limit around a boundary) and per webhook, not per caller IP.
- Public and signed webhooks are found before anything is checked, so their method and environment errors are visible to anyone.
- Refused calls that fail before a webhook is found (404, body too large) are only counted in the metric, not listed.
- **Body schema patterns run on RE2** (session 40; webhooks and AI extract schemas, set by the API and the worker at startup): lookarounds and backreferences are refused everywhere (`patternProblem`); other syntax JavaScript has and RE2 lacks (some `\p{…}` names) compiles in the editor but is refused by the server's check on save. `re2` downloads a prebuilt addon from GitHub on install (compiles with a C++ toolchain otherwise; `RE2_DOWNLOAD_MIRROR`).
- No asymmetric signatures (ed25519, JWT-signed); secret rotation is one secret per line in the config var.
- Custom auth flow steps don't offer the Multipart body in their editor (the connector accepts it).
- Undici's `Response.formData` refuses valid bodies whose boundary text appears inside a part; webhooks use busboy, but REST query tests and the e2e read multipart back with undici (safe: fetch picks random boundaries).

### Resources and connectors
- **Pool invalidation across processes:** no `NOTIFY resource_changed`; pooled clients are keyed by the config's `updatedAt`, so changes take effect on the next load.
- **Row limit (session 40, the user's word):** SQL resources default to `maxRows` 1,000,000 (was 10,000; migration 0041 raised configs still on 10,000). Block data is capped at **50 MB** (`MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`, was 10 MB; migration 0042 replaces the check) and run sandboxes at **512 MB** (was 128 MB), on the user's word. Rows are collected in the process's memory before the cap applies, so a large result costs memory (several times its JSON size) and then fails as `too_large`; the run view loads a block's whole data in the browser; a Python block gets referenced data up to `PYTHON_MAX_REQUEST_MB` (64). The user's Docker VM has 3.8 GB for every service. A resource kept in Git gets its `maxRows` from Git on the next deploy.
- **Postgres `describe()`** leaves one named prepared statement per query on the connection until postgres.js recycles it.
- MySQL evaluates `{{ }}` before the server sees the SQL; MySQL's `NOT IN ({{ [] }})` matches nothing; BigQuery sends null as a NULL STRING (a `CAST` is needed against number columns) and objects as JSON text; no `disablePreparedStatements`.
- The resources playground still uses textareas.
- **Services (M27, M29):** Slack's interactive messages and events, HubSpot's marketing and CMS APIs, SendGrid's contacts, Sheets' formatting only through each service's "Call any …" action; no pickers for HubSpot object types/properties or Airtable/Notion/Salesforce fields; library queries on a service, GraphQL, broker or AI resource are edited as JSON (the library editor knows SQL and REST), and so are the built-in REST API's queries in the app editor; Slack's `blocks` stay in `workflow.json` in Git; the GraphQL variables field has no JSON schema.
- **OpenAPI resources (M29):** schemas expanded one level below the top; parameters are one JSON field; binary non-multipart bodies refused; the document is fetched on demand, not on Test connection.
- **S3 (M29)** has no private-network guard of its own (the SDK makes the requests): its endpoint is admin-only, as SQS's.
- **SSH tunnels (M17, M18):** no SSH agent, certificates, keyboard-interactive/2FA, proxies or shared SSH profiles; no tunnels for BigQuery or the brokers; one pinned host key per hop; a forward to a host the SSH server can't reach waits for that server's TCP timeout (bounded for Postgres only by the query's timeout); PuTTY v3 keys run Argon2 synchronously; Test connection connects the chain twice; tunnels are per process; the server's OAuth authorization-code flows don't use a REST resource's tunnel. A bastion reached through a local `gcloud start-iap-tunnel` only works while that command runs on one person's machine (Optiply's production bastion is only reachable that way today).
- **IAP (M18):** on a Mac the server's identity is a person's (`gcloud auth application-default login`); a service account is the lasting choice. No `nic0` choice, destination groups, private IPs or impersonation. The admin rule watches the server-identity hop's target only. `checkSshIap` builds a token source just to check settings.
- **Revocation and back-off (session 40):** Disconnect revokes at the provider only with a revocation URL (RFC 7009 on the token URL's origin; Google, Slack and Salesforce have one, HubSpot's own `DELETE /oauth/v1/refresh-tokens/:token` and Microsoft aren't supported); accounts dropped because the consent settings changed aren't revoked. A custom flow's sign-in back-off is per process (each API and worker process tries once before backing off) and isn't shown in the resource's page.
- **OAuth and custom auth (M9):** no per-user custom flows (a form step), no admin view of other people's connections (only a count), no status card for custom flows; the process refreshing holds a pooled connection (and the lock) while calling the provider; custom flows' code steps run in a sandbox shared by the process, not the run's.

### Queues and brokers (M19, M26, M30)
- One worker takes every free trigger (no spreading); a trigger's messages are pulled by one worker at a time. Run ends are polled once a second per trigger; the editor's "in flight" can lag up to 5 s.
- Out of scope: Pub/Sub push subscriptions and exactly-once subscriptions, several messages per run, Kafka transactions, Protobuf in the Schema Registry, a dead-letter queue for Kinesis (a failed record is retried, then skipped as `dropped`).
- Kafka's produce query answers per partition (no per-message offsets).
- A dead letter that can't be sent is retried in the worker's memory; a worker stopping meanwhile leaves the message to the next one, which runs it again before dead-lettering it.
- RabbitMQ: no duplicate recognition for messages without a `messageId`.
- Dashboards on `ow_pubsub_*` must use `ow_queue_*{kind="pubsub"}` (renamed in M26).

### Managed database (M7)
- No foreign keys or indexes in the schema editor; no promotion of a schema between environments; no read-only tier.
- The managed tables share the platform's Postgres (statement timeout 120 s, `maxRows` 1M since session 40, 50 connections per role).
- A CSV export holds one of `optiply_db`'s 5 pooled connections per process for the whole download, at the client's pace; a separate pool or a cap on concurrent exports would fix it.
- Plain `SET`s in queries outlast their transaction on pooled connections (every Postgres resource); the Database page pins what it relies on, workflow and playground queries don't.
- The grid edits one cell at a time (no multi-cell paste or undo); deleting rows uses `confirm()`.
- Import problems stop at the first failing batch; the CSV preview reads the first 256 kB.
- With `TEST_DATABASE_URL` (a shared server), test databases leave their `owdb_*` roles behind.

### Apps (M8, M10, M14, M21, M24)
- The watchdog's 5 s also stops legitimate synchronous work (a huge sort in a JS query); Restart starts afresh.
- No themes; no mobile layout (M34 dropped).
- Table: no cell colors or per-row editability; one filter per column from its header; a computed column can't be filtered under server paging; text filters compare by code point in the browser; server-side mode needs a second query for `totalRowCount` (or none); `sql.column` is for SQL resources only.
- Pages: no per-page access (navigation, not security); no page-scoped variables; no middle-click to a new tab; pages reorder with up/down only; deleting a page uses `confirm()`.
- Navigation: one level of groups; icons from a curated set of 38.
- Folders: names visible to everyone listing that kind; a folder holding folders can't be deleted; no view of every folder at once.
- Apps and workflows from before M10 with an entity or block named `url` or `sql` must rename it (the validator says so).
- Editor: REST queries edited as JSON; a component's handle bar covers the lower part of a short component just above; a click on a chart's Table button while the chart isn't selected only selects it.
- Charts: light mode only, no texture fills.
- Automatic queries aren't audited (by design); manual ones are, without values.
- Modules and library queries: a slug can't change; renaming a module input doesn't rewrite a destructured `const { x } = inputs`; the publish warning doesn't show when publishing from a Git branch; the nesting check loads every module release's components (unmeasured beyond tens of modules); library queries can't be called from Python or JavaScript, and have no JavaScript, version ranges or slug renames; `current_user` exists in workflows only for library queries.
- The library editor's REST JSON field isn't shared as text (last write wins); its `gcTime: 0` could become removing the deleted query's cache entry in the delete dialog's `onSuccess`.
- Library query branch drafts through the shared document are covered by e2e specs only, not a server test.

### Custom components (M25, M33)
- npm packages' types are `any` in the editor; the SDK's React declaration is a light one; no custom components as containers, inline components or per-app origins.
- The frame's egress: a CSP doesn't cover a frame navigating itself (stopped after the fact) or WebRTC in every browser.
- Builds run in the API process (two at a time per process); no build queue or metrics.
- Auto height: the editor's canvas keeps the stored height (only the run-time grid follows the content).

### Multiplayer and the editors (M13, M16, M28)
- Two concurrent adds of one name keep the first in document order; concurrent deletes can leave a draft the server won't write ("Not saved") until fixed; a REST `saveDraft` wins over at most a second of collaborative edits.
- The parsed app fields (component props, a variable's initial value, a resource query's JSON, library params) merge as values, not as text.
- The resource playground stays single-user; no comments, following or per-person history.
- Undo/redo remount the side panels (a Monaco field there loses its cursor); undo is per tab and per load.
- Pasting isn't checked against what the person may use (running still needs Use).
- Code with CRLF line ends is stored with `\n` once opened in an editor, so the next Git commit shows those lines changed.
- No "create function from selection".
- Many blocks with forms make a wide canvas (Fit View zooms far out); Switch block type covers single block ↔ loop only.
- Editor completions cover the latest finished run only; no environment choice for them.
- The run panel's logs tab shows the first 500 lines.

### Web generally
- No toast system outside apps (`pages/apps/notifications.tsx`).
- The main bundle was 488 kB at M13 (not re-measured); only the editors and Monaco are split out.
- The label editors' "Add" button takes the whole field label as its accessible name (the M9 e2e relies on it).

### Git sync (M11, M20, M22, M31)
- Hosts other than GitHub and GitLab push and pull without a change-request API (a compare link is shown).
- Commits are authored by the person but pushed with the instance's token or App; the App's key must be RSA.
- Update from branch replaces the draft (no three-way merge of draft edits); Update from main refuses conflicts outside object directories and delete/change conflicts (fix those on the host); its resolutions are per object directory, and resolving one for an object not on the instance is admin-only.
- Diff3, like git, makes changes on adjacent lines one conflict.
- The Git bar's `changes` also fetches the deploy branch (one more fetch per open editor every 30 s); the branch picker reads remote branches at most every 15 s per process.
- GitHub works out a PR's mergeability lazily ("Checking…" for a minute after Update from main).
- An environment Git no longer lists is kept when it holds this instance's secrets, overrides or OAuth accounts; an environment Git names that the instance lacks is skipped silently.
- Deleting objects whose directory left the branch is by hand (flagged `missing`); a folder with `/` in its name (made before M22's review) must be renamed before protecting what's in it.
- Renames: renamed-and-changed on one side reads as deleted and added; library queries have no renamer; a rename is followed only when all three definitions parse.
- Resources have no branch drafts; secrets never come from Git or an outside secret manager.
- The M14 Compose check left 6 `git_objects` rows naming deleted check objects on the user's stack (harmless).

### Python (M12, M23, M32)
- No network from Python and no workflow functions: by design.
- Python's references: a name the code binds anywhere is its own; a block named like a Python builtin can't be read from Python (warning). dict methods win over keys in attribute access (`row["items"]`).
- The executor gets the referenced blocks' data as JSON in each request (up to `PYTHON_MAX_REQUEST_MB`, 64): a copy per Python block.
- Output is cut at 1 MB; logs stream only from Python (JavaScript's went through the store already).
- The editor's syntax markers need the executor (a second after typing stops); the e2e typing helper types Python on one line.
- A first install can outlast a Python block's timeout; no allow or deny list of packages; a workflow's pins win over the image's packages (pinning one pandas needs to an incompatible version breaks pandas for that workflow).
- `python_envs` rows aren't cleaned up (the builder removes unused envs on disk after `PYTHON_ENV_KEEP_DAYS`).
- The editor's packages field and the run timeout are read when the editor mounts (someone else's change shows after a reload).
- An unknown package index isn't a save-time problem (fails installs, runs and publishing as `python_packages`); a root's failed pre-import in a warm env jail isn't logged.

### AI (M35)
- The run's budget is checked before each call: the call crossing it isn't cut off (the next is refused); a resumed run doesn't count a loop's iterations' earlier AI calls.
- Agent tools aren't sent `strict`; tools must be written out (no `{{ }}`); library queries on an AI resource have no agent tools.
- Not checked against the real Anthropic API by hand yet (§12 #44).

### Tests not written
- The OIDC callback logic (extract the claim checks into a pure function).

## 14. Session 60, later: BigQuery with Google OAuth sign-in (branch `feat/bigquery-oauth`)

The user asked whether BigQuery should connect with OAuth/Auth0: Auth0 can't (BigQuery takes only Google-issued tokens),
Google OAuth can; the user said build it on its own branch (architecture §2.45).

1. **Connector and server** ✅ `7788923`: `auth` = service account key (default; old resources unchanged) or Google
   OAuth (shared or each person's own; `projectId` required); every API call's token from the key or the query's

   heredocs) and lets through only read-only commands used read-only (git read subcommands, grep/rg/cat/sed -n/find,

   10:01, finds nothing running and starts it first. Fix (`0cf2952`): under the trigger's lock, a "queue" run is also
   busy while an earlier due **queued** run of its schedule exists (compared as `(available_at, id)` against its own
   row in SQL; `waiting` runs don't count, as before). Deterministic test: another connection holds the 10:00 row
   `for update` while `claimRuns` runs (fails before, passes after). Engine suite 167/167 (+8 skipped), server
   schedule tests 63/63.

    - `fd127ef`: `readOpenSsh` opens a passphrase-protected OpenSSH key **once** and hands ssh2 an unencrypted key in
      memory (through the PEM path, comment kept), as PEM and `.ppk` keys already were; `SshKey` lost `passphrase`.
      Before, ssh2 ran the bcrypt rounds (synchronous, the whole process waiting) on every connection. Storage and
      what the settings show are unchanged. A Test through one hop: 5 openings → 3 (1.4 s → 0.84 s).
    - `4b0191b`: keys asking for more than **128 bcrypt rounds** are refused from the header (`bcryptRounds()`), before
      any work: a key with millions of rounds would freeze the server (anyone with Edit on a resource). PuTTY keys
      already had Argon2 limits.
    Production: 6 of 8 Postgres configs sign in to SSH with a key, all via IAP (a read-only count); whether those
    keys have passphrases is in the encrypted secrets (not looked at): the resource's settings show it.
    Note: a local CPU-load experiment left 16 busy shell loops running once (a hung `wait`); stopped at once, checked
    none left; later load runs used self-ending `node -e` loops.
